# S2-019 - Recovered original Codex conversation

Date: 2026-11-15. Author: Shawn. Tool: OpenAI Codex in VS Code. Issue: #70.

## Provenance and completeness

Recovered directly from the retained primary VS Code session, not from recollection, summaries, or internal approval-reviewer logs. This replaces the earlier excerpt-only submission. The earlier file is retained in Git history and an unchanged local checkpoint outside the repository.

This is the complete available story-scoped user/assistant dialogue through the export cutoff, not a claim to include the entire multi-story project chat. Selected direct-session turns: 55, 164, 165, 166, 173, 174, 175, 176, 177, 178, 179. They cover the early 019 status question, 019 planning/implementation, draft publication, 018/019 dependency discussion, final integration, approved push/description update, acceptance explanation, this transcript-compliance correction, its approved publication, and the latest security-review follow-up. Intervening unrelated 016 reviews and 018-only implementation are excluded as unrelated work.

All 42 original user/assistant messages in these turns are retained in order, without excerpt selection or rewriting apart from explicit privacy redactions. 189 tool call/output records are retained, including failed attempts and corrections. 10 internal approval-log inspection records were omitted/redacted because they are control-plane records, not direct conversation. System/developer instructions, encrypted reasoning, compaction summaries, environment metadata, duplicate event notifications and binary image/audio data are excluded. No original dialogue was reconstructed.

Snapshot cutoff: 2026-11-15T23:02:50.347Z; source snapshot bytes: 78402097; parsed source records: 7882. The final export command is still in progress at the cutoff; subsequent results cannot appear before they exist. A machine-readable count audit is retained locally outside the repository. Paths, non-fixture email addresses, provider-key patterns and bearer/JWT values are privacy-redacted.

## Accepted, changed, rejected

Accepted: fresh session-authorized target context, account/target-scoped UI, Activity cleanup, request-identity guards, complete provider-request tests, current-main integration, and review/CI before merge. Changed: independent pending state, user reset key, an ingestion-status test selector after baseline reproduction, normal merge of finalized 016, and this direct-session recovery. Rejected: another context cache/coordinator, fabricated dialogue, treating fake infrastructure as hosted/live verification, claiming an unapproved standards exception, or changing GitHub without confirmation.

    "text": "{\"chunk_id\":\"82a47a\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.3722635,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":8803,\"output\":\"---\\r\\ntitle: How Next.js preserves UI state with Activity\\r\\nnav_title: Preserving UI state\\r\\ndescription: Learn how React's Activity component preserves UI state across navigations in Next.js and how to control what resets.\\r\\nrelated:\\r\\n  title: Related\\r\\n  description: Learn more about Cache Components and preserving UI state.\\r\\n  links:\\r\\n    - app/getting-started/caching\\r\\n    - app/guides/migrating-to-cache-components\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n> **Good to know:** This guide assumes [Cache Components](/docs/app/getting-started/caching) is enabled. Enable it by setting [`cacheComponents: true`](/docs/app/api-reference/config/next-config-js/cacheComponents) in your Next config file.\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore Cache Components, preserving page-level state across navigations required workarounds like hoisting state to a [shared layout](/docs/app/getting-started/layouts-and-pages#nesting-layouts) or using an external store. With Cache Components, Next.js preserves state and DOM out of the box.\\r\\n\\r\\nInstead of unmounting pages on navigation, Next.js hides them using React's [`<Activity>`](https://react.dev/reference/react/Activity) component. Activity keeps the DOM in the document (hidden with `display: none`), so both React state and DOM state are preserved: form drafts, scroll positions, expanded `<details>` elements, video playback progress, and more.\\r\\n\\r\\nNext.js preserves up to 3 routes. Beyond that, the oldest route is evicted and will re-render fresh.\\r\\n\\r\\n> **Good to know:** Use [`useRouter().bfcacheId`](/docs/app/api-reference/functions/use-router#bfcacheid) as a [React `key`](https://react.dev/learn/preserving-and-resetting-state#option-2-resetting-state-with-a-key): a single `<Fragment key={bfcacheId}>` resets an entire subtree on push or replace navigations (including `<Link>` clicks and `router.push` / `router.replace`) while still restoring state on browser back/forward. `bfcacheId` is mainly a migration tool. For new code, prefer the per-pattern resets below.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Choosing what to preserve\\r\\n\\r\\nActivity preserves all component state and DOM state by default. For each piece of state, you decide whether that's the right behavior for your UI. The patterns below show common scenarios and how to handle both sides.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Expandable UI (dropdowns, accordions, panels)\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen a user navigates away and returns, Activity preserves the open/closed state of expandable elements.\\r\\n\\r\\n**When to keep it:** A sidebar with expanded sections, a FAQ accordion, or a filters panel. The user set up their view intentionally, and restoring it avoids re-doing that work.\\r\\n\\r\\n**When to reset it:** A dropdown menu or popover triggered by a button click. These are transient interactions, not persistent view state. Returning to a page with a dropdown already open is not user friendly.\\r\\n\\r\\nTo reset transient open/closed state, close it in a `useLayoutEffect` cleanup function:\\r\\n\\r\\n```tsx highlight={8-13}\\r\\n'use client'\\r\\n\\r\\nimport { useState, useLayoutEffect } from 'react'\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction SettingsDropdown() {\\r\\n  const [isOpen, setIsOpen] = useState(false)\\r\\n\\r\\n  // Close dropdown when this component becomes hidden\\r\\n  useLayoutEffect(() => {\\r\\n    return () => {\\r\\n      setIsOpen(false)\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }, [])\\r\\n\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <div>\\r\\n      <button onClick={() => setIsOpen((o) => !o)}>Options</button>\\r\\n      {isOpen && (\\r\\n        <ul>\\r\\n          <li>\\r\\n            <button>Edit Profile</button>\\r\\n          </li>\\r\\n          <li>\\r\\n            <button>Change Password</button>\\r\\n          </li>\\r\\n        </ul>\\r\\n      )}\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  )\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen Activity hides this component, the cleanup function runs and resets `isOpen`. When the page becomes visible again, the dropdown is closed. Using `useLayoutEffect` ensures the cleanup runs synchronously before the component is hidden, avoiding any flash of stale state.\\r\\n\\r\\nYou can also use `Link`'s [`onNavigate`](/docs/app/api-reference/components/link#onnavigate) callback to close dropdowns immediately when a navigation link is clicked.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Dialog and initialization logic\\r\\n\\r\\nActivity preserves dialog open/closed state. This also affects Effects that run based on that state.\\r\\n\\r\\n**When to keep it:** A multi-step wizard or a settings panel that the user was actively working in. Preserving the step and input state avoids losing progress.\\r\\n\\r\\n**When to reset it:** A dialog that runs initialization logic (like focusing an input) each time it opens. If the user navigated away while the dialog was open, Activity preserves `isDialogOpen: true`. Opening it again sets it to `true` when it's already `true`, so no state change happens and the Effect doesn't re-run.\\r\\n\\r\\nConsider this example:\\r\\n\\r\\n```tsx\\r\\n'use client'\\r\\n\\r\\nimport { useState, useRef, useEffect } from 'react'\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction ProductTab() {\\r\\n  const [isDialogOpen, setIsDialogOpen] = useState(false)\\r\\n  const inputRef = useRef<HTMLInputElement>(null)\\r\\n\\r\\n  useEffect(() => {\\r\\n    if (isDialogOpen) {\\r\\n      inputRef.current?.focus()\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }, [isDialogOpen])\\r\\n\\r\\n  // ...\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nIf the user navigated away while the dialog was open, returning and opening the dialog won't trigger the focus Effect because `isDialogOpen` was already `true`.\\r\\n\\r\\nTo fix this, derive the dialog state from something outside the preserved component state like a search param:\\r\\n\\r\\n```tsx highlight={3,7-9,20,25}\\r\\n'use client'\\r\\n\\r\\nimport { useSearchParams, useRouter } from 'next/navigation'\\r\\nimport { useEffect, useRef } from 'react'\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction ProductTab() {\\r\\n  const searchParams = useSearchParams()\\r\\n  const router = useRouter()\\r\\n  const isDialogOpen = searchParams.get('edit') === 'true'\\r\\n  const inputRef = useRef<HTMLInputElement>(null)\\r\\n\\r\\n  useEffect(() => {\\r\\n    if (isDialogOpen) {\\r\\n      inputRef.current?.focus()\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }, [isDialogOpen])\\r\\n\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <div>\\r\\n      <button onClick={() => router.push('?edit=true')}>Edit Product</button>\\r\\n\\r\\n      {isDialogOpen && (\\r\\n        <dialog open>\\r\\n          <input ref={inputRef} placeholder=\\\"Product name\\\" />\\r\\n          <button onClick={() => router.replace('?', { scroll: false })}>\\r\\n            Close\\r\\n          </button>\\r\\n        </dialog>\\r\\n      )}\\r\\n    </div>\\r\\n  )\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nWith this approach, `isDialogOpen` derives from the URL rather than component state. When navigating away and returning, the search param is cleared (the URL changed), so `isDialogOpen` becomes `false`. Opening the dialog sets the param, which changes `isDialogOpen` and triggers the Effect.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Forms, inputs, and state\\r\\n\\r\\nActivity preserves form input values (text fields, selected options, checkbox states), submission results, and status messages across navigations.\\r\\n\\r\\n**When to keep it:** A search page with filters, a draft the user was composing, or a settings form with unsaved changes. Preserving input state is one of the biggest UX wins because the user doesn't lose work.\\r\\n\\r\\n**When to reset it:** A \\\"new transaction\\\" flow where each visit should start fresh, or a form where stale success/error messages would be confusing in a new context.\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Resetting form state on submit\\r\\n\\r\\nConsider a page where the user creates a new item. After submitting, `router.push` navigates to the new record. Since Activity preserves the page, navigating back shows the previous name still in the form. Reset state in the event handler to keep the form fresh:\\r\\n\\r\\n```tsx filename=\\\"app/new/page.tsx\\\" highlight={13}\\r\\n'use client'\\r\\n\\r\\nimport { useState } from 'react'\\r\\nimport { useRouter } from 'next/navigation'\\r\\n\\r\\nexport default function NewItemPage() {\\r\\n  const [name, setName] = useState('')\\r\\n  const router = useRouter()\\r\\n\\r\\n  async function handleSubmit(e: React.FormEvent) {\\r\\n    e.preventDefault()\\r\\n    const item = await createItem({ name })\\r\\n    setName('')\\r\\n    router.push(`/items/${item.id}`)\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <form onSubmit={handleSubmit}>\\r\\n      <input value={name} onChange={(e) => setName(e.target.value)} />\\r\\n      <button type=\\\"submit\\\">Create</button>\\r\\n    </form>\\r\\n  )\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n#### Resetting stale status messages\\r\\n\\r\\nIf after submitting you set a status into state to render a feedback message, there's often not a reliable user-initiated event to clear it.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe user might navigate via `next/link` elements out of your control, or via browser controls.\\r\\n\\r\\nNavigating back to the form shows a stale message. In this case, you may use a `useLayoutEffect` cleanup to reset the form and state:\\r\\n\\r\\n```tsx highlight={17-26}\\r\\n'use client'\\r\\n\\r\\nimport { useState, useRef, useLayoutEffect } from 'react'\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction ContactForm() {\\r\\n  const [name, setName] = useState('')\\r\\n  const [status, setStatus] = useState<'idle' | 'success'>('idle')\\r\\n  const shouldReset = useRef(false)\\r\\n\\r\\n  async function handleSubmit(e: React.FormEvent) {\\r\\n    e.preventDefault()\\r\\n    await sendMessage({ name })\\r\\n    setStatus('success')\\r\\n    shouldReset.current = true\\r\\n  }\\r\\n\\r\\n  // Reset stale success message when Activity hides this component\\r\\n  useLayoutEffect(() => {\\r\\n    return () => {\\r\\n      if (shouldReset.current) {\\r\\n        shouldReset.current = false\\r\\n        setStatus('idle')\\r\\n        setName('')\\r\\n      }\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }, [])\\r\\n\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <form onSubmit={handleSubmit}>\\r\\n      <input value={name} onChange={(e) => setName(e.target.value)} />\\r\\n      <button type=\\\"submit\\\">Send</button>\\r\\n      {status === 'success' && <p>Message sent!</p>}\\r\\n    </form>\\r\\n  )\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThe `shouldReset` ref ensures the cleanup only runs after a successful submission. If the user navigates away mid-draft without submitting, their input is preserved.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf you use [`useActionState`](https://react.dev/reference/react/useActionState), the same approach applies. See [Reset state](https://react.dev/reference/react/useActionState#reset-state) in the React docs for how to add a `RESET` action to your reducer. For more on building responsive interactions with these hooks, see the [Building interactive apps](/docs/app/guides/interactive-apps) guide.\\r\\n\\r\\n<details>\\r\\n<summary>Resetting all form fields with a callback ref</summary>\\r\\n\\r\\nYou can use a callback ref to call `form.reset()` when Activity hides the component:\\r\\n\\r\\n```tsx\\r\\n<form\\r\\n  ref={(form) => {\\r\\n    return () => form?.reset()\\r\\n  }}\\r\\n>\\r\\n  <input name=\\\"email\\\" />\\r\\n  <input name=\\\"message\\\" />\\r\\n  <button type=\\\"submit\\\">Send</button>\\r\\n</form>\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThis resets all fields whenever the user navigates away.\\r\\n\\r\\n</details>\\r\\n\\r\\n## State and authentication\\r\\n\\r\\nActivity preserves local component state (`useState`, DOM input values) across navigations, including authentication changes. This is standard React behavior: props changing (such as receiving a new user) triggers a re-render but does not reset existing state. A draft composed by one user shouldn't be visible to another.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor logout flows, using `window.location.href` instead of `router.push` triggers a full page reload, clearing all client-side state.\\r\\n\\r\\nTo reset specific state when the user changes without a full reload:\\r\\n\\r\\n```tsx\\r\\n'use client'\\r\\n\\r\\nimport { useState, useEffect, useRef } from 'react'\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction UserScopedForm({ userId }: { userId: string | null }) {\\r\\n  const [draft, setDraft] = useState('')\\r\\n  const lastUserIdRef = useRef<string | null>(null)\\r\\n\\r\\n  useEffect(() => {\\r\\n    if (lastUserIdRef.current !== null && lastUserIdRef.current !== userId) {\\r\\n      setDraft('') // Reset on user change\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    lastUserIdRef.current = userId\\r\\n  }, [userId])\\r\\n\\r\\n  return <textarea value={draft} onChange={(e) => setDraft(e.target.value)} />\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nAlternatively, key components by user ID to let React handle the reset: `<Form key={userId} />`.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Global styles\\r\\n\\r\\nPage-level styles (CSS variables, z-index, global classes) can affect visible pages when the originating component is hidden by Activity. You likely want to disable them when hidden: a hidden page's accent color or z-index overrides shouldn't leak into the visible page.\\r\\n\\r\\nUse a callback ref to toggle the stylesheet's `media` attribute:\\r\\n\\r\\n```tsx\\r\\n<style\\r\\n  ref={(style) => {\\r\\n    if (style) style.media = '' // Enable when visible\\r\\n    return () => {\\r\\n      if (style) style.media = 'not all' // Disable when hidden\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }}\\r\\n>\\r\\n  {`:root { --page-accent: blue; }`}\\r\\n</style>\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nOr use `useLayoutEffect` when managing multiple style elements or more complex cleanup:\\r\\n\\r\\n```tsx\\r\\n'use client'\\r\\n\\r\\nimport { useLayoutEffect, useRef } from 'react'\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction PageWithStyles() {\\r\\n  const styleRef = useRef<HTMLStyleElement>(null)\\r\\n\\r\\n  useLayoutEffect(() => {\\r\\n    if (styleRef.current) styleRef.current.media = ''\\r\\n    return () => {\\r\\n      if (styleRef.current) styleRef.current.media = 'not all'\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }, [])\\r\\n\\r\\n  return <style ref={styleRef}>{`:root { --page-accent: blue; }`}</style>\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen Activity hides the component, the cleanup sets `media=\\\"not all\\\"`, which disables the stylesheet. When visible again, the effect re-runs and resets `media` to enable it.\\r\\n\\r\\n### The `:has` selector\\r\\n\\r\\nA [`:root:has(...)`](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/Reference/Selectors/:has) rule applies styles based on its selector. Neither the selected element nor the matched element knows about the relationship. It's valid CSS, but it bypasses React's data flow and couples unrelated components.\\r\\n\\r\\nFor global state, prefer a `data-*` attribute that React owns:\\r\\n\\r\\n```tsx\\r\\n<html data-modal-open={modalOpen ? \\\"true\\\" : undefined}>\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n```css\\r\\nhtml[data-modal-open='true'] {\\r\\n  overflow: hidden;\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nReserve `:has()` for local parent/child styling within a component:\\r\\n\\r\\n```css\\r\\n.card:has(img) {\\r\\n  padding-top: 0;\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThis isn't only a React data-flow or Next.js concern. Broad `:has()` selectors are a real performance bottleneck. See [Performance considerations](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/Reference/Selectors/:has#performance_considerations) on MDN.\\r\\n\\r\\n> **Good to know:** If the hidden component itself defines the global `:has` rule, the toggle from the section above disables it along with the component's other styles.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Testing\\r\\n\\r\\nHidden Activity content has `display: none` but remains in the document. This applies both to routes preserved by Cache Components and to content you hide with `<Activity>` directly. It affects end-to-end testing with tools like Playwright, Cypress, or Puppeteer:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **DOM queries can find hidden elements.** Selectors may match elements regardless of visibility.\\r\\n- **Interactions with hidden elements fail or timeout.** Most tools wait for elements to become visible before interacting.\\r\\n- **Assertions may match hidden content.** Be explicit about visibility when asserting element presence.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Use visibility-aware selectors\\r\\n\\r\\nIn Playwright, `getByRole` queries automatically filter by visibility:\\r\\n\\r\\n```ts\\r\\n// Good - getByRole filters by visibility automatically\\r\\nawait page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Submit' }).click()\\r\\nawait page.getByRole('textbox', { name: 'Email' }).fill('test@example.com')\\r\\n\\r\\n// Also good - getByLabel, getByPlaceholder filter by visibility\\r\\nawait page.getByLabel('Email').fill('test@example.com')\\r\\nawait page.getByPlaceholder('Search...').fill('query')\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen `getByRole` isn't suitable, use `.locator()` with visibility filtering:\\r\\n\\r\\n```ts\\r\\n// Fallback - filter by visibility explicitly\\r\\nawait page.locator('.product-card').filter({ visible: true }).first().click()\\r\\nawait page\\r\\n  .locator('[data-testid=\\\"timer\\\"]')\\r\\n  .filter({ visible: true })\\r\\n  .textContent()\\r\\n\\r\\n// Avoid - may match hidden elements in Activity boundaries\\r\\nawait page.locator('.product-card').first().click()\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n`getByRole` is robust to Activity, tabbed navigation, accordions, and any other pattern that keeps hidden content in the DOM. It queries the accessibility tree, which excludes hidden elements. For other testing tools, check their documentation for visibility-aware selectors. For example, Cypress uses `.should('be.visible')` or `{ visible: true }` options.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Using Activity in your components\\r\\n\\r\\nCache Components uses Activity automatically at the route level, but you can also use `<Activity>` directly in your own components. This is useful for tabs, expandable panels, or any UI where you want to hide content without unmounting it.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Prerendering hidden content\\r\\n\\r\\nActivity can prerender content the user hasn't seen yet. Hidden boundaries render at lower priority. Combined with Suspense, this lets you prefetch data for content the user is likely to view next.\\r\\n\\r\\nA Server Component can start fetching data immediately and pass the promise to a client component. The client component uses Activity to hide the content until the user requests it, and `use()` to resolve the promise when rendering:\\r\\n\\r\\n```tsx filename=\\\"app/page.tsx\\\"\\r\\nimport { Suspense } from 'react'\\r\\nimport { ExpandableComments } from './expandable-comments'\\r\\n\\r\\nasync function getCommentsData() {\\r\\n  return db.comments.findMany()\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport default function Page() {\\r\\n  const commentsPromise = getCommentsData()\\r\\n\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <article>\\r\\n      <h1>Post Title</h1>\\r\\n      <p>Main content visible immediately...</p>\\r\\n\\r\\n      <ExpandableComments commentsPromise={commentsPromise} />\\r\\n    </article>\\r\\n  )\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n```tsx filename=\\\"app/expandable-comments.tsx\\\"\\r\\n'use client'\\r\\n\\r\\nimport { Activity, Suspense, useState, use } from 'react'\\r\\n\\r\\ntype Comment = { id: string; text: string; author: string }\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function ExpandableComments({\\r\\n  commentsPromise,\\r\\n}: {\\r\\n  commentsPromise: Promise<Comment[]>\\r\\n}) {\\r\\n  const [expanded, setExpanded] = useState(false)\\r\\n\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <>\\r\\n      <button onClick={() => setExpanded((e) => !e)}>\\r\\n        {expanded ? 'Hide Comments' : 'Show Comments'}\\r\\n      </button>\\r\\n\\r\\n      <Activity mode={expanded ? 'visible' : 'hidden'}>\\r\\n        <Suspense fallback={<CommentsSkeleton />}>\\r\\n          <Comments commentsPromise={commentsPromise} />\\r\\n        </Suspense>\\r\\n      </Activity>\\r\\n    </>\\r\\n  )\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction Comments({\\r\\n  commentsPromise,\\r\\n}: {\\r\\n  commentsPromise: Promise<Comment[]>\\r\\n}) {\\r\\n  const comments = use(commentsPromise)\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <ul>\\r\\n      {comments.map((c) => (\\r\\n        <li key={c.id}>{c.text}</li>\\r\\n      ))}\\r\\n    </ul>\\r\\n  )\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction CommentsSkeleton() {\\r\\n  return <div>Loading comments...</div>\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThe Server Component starts fetching comments immediately and passes the promise down. While hidden, the data streams at lower priority. When the user clicks \\\"Show Comments\\\", the `Comments` component resolves the promise with `use()` and the content appears instantly.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Effect and media cleanup\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen Activity hides content, React runs effect cleanup functions just like it does on unmount. This means timers, subscriptions, and media playback pause automatically if you have proper cleanup:\\r\\n\\r\\n```tsx\\r\\n'use client'\\r\\n\\r\\nimport { useEffect, useState } from 'react'\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction LiveTimer() {\\r\\n  const [count, setCount] = useState(0)\\r\\n\\r\\n  useEffect(() => {\\r\\n    const id = setInterval(() => setCount((c) => c + 1), 1000)\\r\\n    return () => clearInterval(id) // Pauses when hidden\\r\\n  }, [])\\r\\n\\r\\n  return <p>Count: {count}</p>\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nFor media elements like `<video>` and `<audio>`, `display: none` does not stop playback. Add explicit cleanup with `useLayoutEffect`:\\r\\n\\r\\n```tsx\\r\\n'use client'\\r\\n\\r\\nimport { useLayoutEffect, useRef } from 'react'\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction VideoPlayer({ src }: { src: string }) {\\r\\n  const videoRef = useRef<HTMLVideoElement>(null)\\r\\n\\r\\n  useLayoutEffect(() => {\\r\\n    const video = videoRef.current\\r\\n    return () => {\\r\\n      video?.pause() // Pauses when hidden, preserves playback position\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }, [])\\r\\n\\r\\n  return <video ref={videoRef} src={src} controls />\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen the component becomes visible again, effects re-run and playback position is preserved since the DOM node was never removed.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Distinguishing first mount from re-show\\r\\n\\r\\nEffects run on every hide-to-visible transition, not just the initial mount. If you need to distinguish the first mount from subsequent visibility changes, use a ref:\\r\\n\\r\\n```tsx\\r\\n'use client'\\r\\n\\r\\nimport { useEffect, useRef } from 'react'\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction TrackedComponent() {\\r\\n  const hasMountedRef = useRef(false)\\r\\n\\r\\n  useEffect(() => {\\r\\n    if (!hasMountedRef.current) {\\r\\n      hasMountedRef.current = true\\r\\n      console.log('First mount')\\r\\n    } else {\\r\\n      console.log('Became visible again')\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }, [])\\r\\n\\r\\n  return <div>...</div>\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThe ref persists across hide/show cycles (refs aren't cleaned up), so `hasMountedRef.current` stays `true` after the first mount. Each time Activity becomes visible, the Effect runs again, but now it takes the `else` branch.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Examples\\r\\n\\r\\nThe [Activity Patterns Demo](https://react-activity-patterns.labs.vercel.dev) ([source](https://github.com/nextjs/react-activity-patterns)) is a Next.js app with Cache Components enabled and three routes. Navigate between them to see state preservation in action:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Data** â€” sortable table and selectable list that keep their state across navigations, plus a reviews section that prerenders in the background\\r\\n- **Forms** â€” filter panel with DOM state (`<details>`, checkboxes, text inputs) that persists, and a newsletter form that resets after submission using `useLayoutEffect` cleanup\\r\\n- **Side Effects** â€” a live timer that pauses when you navigate away and resumes when you return, and a video player that auto-pauses with playback position preserved\\r\\n\\\"use client\\\";\\r\\n\\r\\nimport { useEffect, useId, useRef, useState } from \\\"react\\\";\\r\\nimport { useRouter } from \\\"next/navigation\\\";\\r\\nimport { useForm } from \\\"react-hook-form\\\";\\r\\nimport { zodResolver } from \\\"@hookform/resolvers/zod\\\";\\r\\nimport { CircleAlert } from \\\"lucide-react\\\";\\r\\nimport { Card } from \\\"@/components/ui/Card\\\";\\r\\nimport { Button } from \\\"@/components/ui/Button\\\";\\r\\nimport { Input } from \\\"@/components/ui/Input\\\";\\r\\nimport {\\r\\n  Form,\\r\\n  FormField,\\r\\n  FormItem,\\r\\n  FormLabel,\\r\\n  FormControl,\\r\\n  FormMessage,\\r\\n} from \\\"@/components/ui/Form\\\";\\r\\nimport { Alert, AlertTitle, AlertDescription } from \\\"@/components/ui/Alert\\\";\\r\\nimport { Spinner } from \\\"@/components/shared/Spinner\\\";\\r\\nimport {\\r\\n  askReviewQuestion,\\r\\n  ReviewQuestionError,\\r\\n  type ReviewAnswer,\\r\\n} from \\\"@/lib/api/review-questions\\\";\\r\\nimport { reviewQuestionSchema } from \\\"@/lib/validation/review-question\\\";\\r\\n\\r\\ntype Props = {\\r\\n  targetId: string;\\r\\n  targetName: string;\\r\\n  reviewsAvailable: boolean;\\r\\n};\\r\\n\\r\\nconst outcomeLabels = {\\r\\n  answered: \\\"ReviewLens response\\\",\\r\\n  insufficient_evidence: \\\"Insufficient review evidence\\\",\\r\\n  out_of_scope: \\\"Outside the active reviews\\\",\\r\\n};\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function ReviewQuestions(props: Props) {\\r\\n  const { bfcacheId } = useRouter();\\r\\n  // A new target or navigation restores a fresh form, never another target's answer.\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <QuestionForm key={`${props.targetId}:${bfcacheId}:${props.reviewsAvailable}`} {...props} />\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction QuestionForm({ targetId, targetName, reviewsAvailable }: Props) {\\r\\n  const router = useRouter();\\r\\n  const questionHelpId = useId();\\r\\n  const request = useRef<AbortController | null>(null);\\r\\n  const [answer, setAnswer] = useState<ReviewAnswer | null>(null);\\r\\n  const [answeredQuestion, setAnsweredQuestion] = useState(\\\"\\\");\\r\\n  const [error, setError] = useState<string | null>(null);\\r\\n  const form = useForm<{ question: string }>({\\r\\n    resolver: zodResolver(reviewQuestionSchema),\\r\\n    defaultValues: { question: \\\"\\\" },\\r\\n  });\\r\\n\\r\\n  useEffect(\\r\\n    () => () => {\\r\\n      request.current?.abort();\\r\\n      request.current = null;\\r\\n    },\\r\\n    [],\\r\\n  );\\r\\n\\r\\n  const submit = async ({ question }: { question: string }) => {\\r\\n    if (request.current || !reviewsAvailable) return;\\r\\n    const controller = new AbortController();\\r\\n    request.current = controller;\\r\\n    // The provider now has separate 30s counting and generation deadlines.\\r\\n    const timeout = setTimeout(() => controller.abort(), 75_000);\\r\\n    setAnswer(null);\\r\\n    setError(null);\\r\\n    try {\\r\\n      const result = await askReviewQuestion(targetId, question, controller.signal);\\r\\n      if (controller.signal.aborted) return;\\r\\n      setAnsweredQuestion(question);\\r\\n      setAnswer(result);\\r\\n    } catch (cause) {\\r\\n      // Unmount aborts must not affect the newly selected workspace.\\r\\n      if (request.current !== controller) return;\\r\\n      if (cause instanceof ReviewQuestionError && cause.fieldErrors?.question) {\\r\\n        form.setError(\\r\\n          \\\"question\\\",\\r\\n          { type: \\\"server\\\", message: cause.fieldErrors.question },\\r\\n          { shouldFocus: true },\\r\\n        );\\r\\n        return;\\r\\n      }\\r\\n      if (cause instanceof ReviewQuestionError && cause.code === \\\"AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED\\\") {\\r\\n        router.replace(`/login?next=${encodeURIComponent(`/analysis-targets/${targetId}`)}`);\\r\\n      }\\r\\n      setError(\\r\\n        cause instanceof ReviewQuestionError\\r\\n          ? cause.message\\r\\n          : \\\"We couldn't answer your question. Please try again.\\\",\\r\\n      );\\r\\n    } finally {\\r\\n      clearTimeout(timeout);\\r\\n      request.current = null;\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  };\\r\\n\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <Card className=\\\"space-y-4\\\" aria-label=\\\"Review questions\\\">\\r\\n      <h2 className=\\\"text-xl font-semibold\\\">Ask ReviewLens</h2>\\r\\n      <p id={questionHelpId} className=\\\"max-w-prose break-words text-sm text-muted-foreground\\\">\\r\\n        Active target: <span className=\\\"font-medium text-foreground\\\">{targetName}</span> Â· Google\\r\\n        Maps. ### 2026-11-15T18:46:04.332Z - assistant (source turn 165, record 6624)

I found a lifecycle gap worth fixing: Next.js preserves hidden pages and their form state during back/forward navigation. The current form aborts its request, but retains its answer, draft and submission state. I’m adding tests for that behavior and for an old request finishing after a new one starts, then fixing the form lifecycle.

### 2026-11-15T18:52:24.596Z - assistant (source turn 165, record 6725)

The production-build browser checks are passing so far, including back/forward restoration and delayed old-target responses. The full Jest run also passed: 1,471 tests. I’ve since added two more account/lifecycle cases and will include them in the final run.

One limit to be clear about: aborting a browser request cannot recall data already sent to a provider. The checks verify that its late response cannot overwrite the active workspace and that the next request builds fresh, authorized context.

text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"$code = @'\nconst fs=require('fs');\nfs.writeFileSync('docs/s2-019-prevent-stale-context.md',String.raw`# S2-019: Prevent stale review context\n\nIssue: [#70](https://github.com/aum-2004/CS490_Review_Lens_AI_Fantastic_Six/issues/70).\nAuthor: Shawn. Local verification: 2026-11-15, based on main at 2a9e54b6.\n\n## Behavior and requirements\n\nEvery question uses the current route's target ID and newly authenticated persisted\nreview context. No review evidence, owner claim, previous answer or provider thread\nis accepted from the browser. S2-019 adds lifecycle protection to the existing Q&A\nform rather than a second context store or parallel request coordinator.\n\n| Requirement | Implementation and evidence |\n| --- | --- |\n| S2-BR-022: only the authenticated user's reviews enter AI context | The unchanged S2-010 builder re-verifies session and ownership before reading reviews. Real-builder/adapter tests switch accounts, deny the previous account's target and inspect both counting and generation bodies. |\n| S2-BR-023: target changes change subsequent Q&A datasets | A -> B -> A tests inspect exact review IDs, text, ratings and dates in each outgoing body. Browser tests submit from each active workspace. |\n| S2-BR-024: previously selected target reviews do not remain active | No cross-request context cache or provider conversation history. Form lifecycle cleanup clears answers, question drafts, errors and pending state. Old successes, failures and authentication errors cannot affect a restored/current request. |\n| Explicit transitions rather than isolated targets | Tests cover A -> B -> A, account changes, overlapping requests completing in reverse order, hide/restore, browser back/forward, reload and logout while pending. |\n| Cache boundaries | No new application context cache, database Q&A history or retrieval index. Requests use no-store; generation uses store:false and omits conversation/previous_response_id. Next Activity is handled explicitly below. |\n\n## Client lifecycle\n\nInstalled Next 16.4.3 documentation explains that Cache Components preserves hidden\npages with React Activity, including state and DOM. bfcacheId changes on fresh\nnavigation but is restored on back/forward; it is not a logout or refresh epoch.\n\nReviewQuestions keys its form by the server-verified user ID, target ID,\nnavigation identity and review availability. The user ID is a UI reset key, not an\nauthorization credential; it is never sent as an owner override in the Q&A request.\nProtectedTargetWorkspace supplies it from requirePageUser(), after server identity\nverification. Backend checks remain authoritative.\n\nA layout-effect cleanup aborts the old fetch and clears transient form state when\nActivity hides the page or the component unmounts. The UI tracks the current\nrequest's pending state separately from react-hook-form's asynchronous submission\nstate. An old promise's finally block cannot unlock or clear a newer request after\nrestoration. Success and error handling both check request ownership.\n\nNavigating away deliberately discards Q&A drafts and answers. Returning begins a\nfresh question; it does not restore historical answers as current analysis.\n\n## Verification\n\n- Full Jest with coverage: 80 suites / 1,473 tests passed, zero skips.\n- Changed-code gate against origin/main: 32/32 changed production lines covered;\n  7/7 changed branch outcomes covered. Overall full-suite line coverage: 94.11%.\n- Focused component + real pipeline + production-route integration: 70 tests passed.\n- Production build, ESLint, explicit TypeScript check and formatting passed.\n- Browser evidence is in tests/e2e/auth/stale-review-context.spec.ts: real Next\n  production build and local fake Supabase, intercepted answer responses, Edge.\n  Provider-boundary evidence is separate: real builder, answer service and adapter,\n  synthetic model configuration, fake HTTP and network guard. No paid calls.\n- Existing production-route integration also runs in the full suite. It checks\n  session/ownership failures, context failures, private responses, budget and quota.\n- Three restored source-fault challenges were caught: retaining an old answer\n  (1 failure), allowing old finally to unlock a new request (3 failures), and\n  dropping the user reset key (2 failures). Sources were restored and focused\n  tests passed again afterward.\n\nThe broader browser run exposed an existing target-creation assertion failure,\nreproduced on an unchanged main-equivalent production build. The workspace has two\nlegitimate Not ingested labels (target details and ingestion panel). The test now\nasserts the ingestion panel's status specifically rather than counting the entire\npage. No product status behavior was changed for that correction.\n\n## Compatibility and handoff\n\nS2-010's buildReviewDatasetContext(targetId), S2-011 answerQuestion(), the questions\nroute, response envelope, token counting, durable quota and provider configuration\nare unchanged. This story has no migration, dependency or environment change.\nQ&A remains off by default. Existing S2-003 fixtures and harness are reused.\n\nA separate, unpublished composition of S2-006 533b78f8 and S2-017 b46f6ecd with\nthis implementation retained summary, target selector and Q&A. TypeScript and\n47 focused tests passed after including each story's updated test fixtures.\nThis is compatibility evidence, not an approval of those PRs or proof of an\nautomatic merge. Their shared workspace reconciliation must preserve\nuserId={user.id} on ReviewQuestions as well as summary and selector functionality.\nNo other story's production code is included in this delivery branch. S2-018's\nolder isolated foundation is preserved separately.\n\n## Boundaries and remaining review gates\n\nAn already dispatched provider request cannot be recalled by aborting a browser\nfetch. Its completion stays scoped to the original request and cannot update the\nnew workspace. Subsequent questions independently rebuild authorized context.\nstore:false avoids application conversation storage; it does not assert that a\nprovider retains no operational data under its own policies.\n\nTests use fake infrastructure: they do not certify hosted RLS deployment, live\nmodel semantic grounding, provider retention policy or an instantaneous logout\nbroadcast to an already-visible second browser tab. Every subsequent Q&A request\nstill re-verifies identity. Same-browser supported logout/login and account prop\nchanges are covered explicitly.\n\nBefore merge: run current-head GitHub CI, obtain normal and independent\nsecurity-focused reviews under AI-Assisted-Development section 7, and complete team\nacceptance. No remote review, PR edit, push, merge or issue closure was performed\nby this local implementation session. Keep #70 open until those gates are met.\n\nAI assistance: [scoped original-message record and verification notes](../ai-transcripts/2026-11-15_shawn_S2-019_codex.md).\n`);\nfs.writeFileSync('ai-transcripts/2026-11-15_shawn_S2-019_codex.md',String.raw`# S2-019 — Codex implementation session\n\nDate: 2026-11-15. Author: Shawn. Tool: OpenAI Codex.\nIssue: #70. Base: main 2a9e54b6. Branch: feature/S2-019-prevent-stale-context.\n\n## Scope and provenance\n\nThis is a scoped record of the S2-019 request, original user-facing response\nexcerpts and verification/correction notes. It is not a full export of the long\nproject chat, hidden reasoning or every tool invocation. Earlier story reviews\nand unrelated personal information are excluded. Full direct-session export was\nnot available through the tools used here. Notes below are labeled as notes and\nare not reconstructed dialogue. Paths in verification excerpts are omitted.\n\n## Original user request\n\n> let's complete 019, make sure all requirements are satisfied, try not to let issues get by, and make sure it's compatible with our work so far. if anything is uncertain or doubtful, just let me know\n\n## Original assistant response excerpts\n\n> I’ll complete 019 against current main, trace where review context and answers can persist, and test target changes, late responses, logout/account changes and browser history. I’ll report any gaps or uncertainty explicitly. All work will stay local; nothing will be pushed or posted without your confirmation.\n\n> I found a lifecycle gap worth fixing: Next.js preserves hidden pages and their form state during back/forward navigation. The current form aborts its request, but retains its answer, draft and submission state. I’m adding tests for that behavior and for an old request finishing after a new one starts, then fixing the form lifecycle.\n\n> The new tests reproduced the preserved-page problems, and the form fixes now pass. I also tested the real context builder, answer service and OpenAI adapter with fake network responses: A → B → A and account changes use only the current authorized reviews, even when requests finish out of order.\n>\n> I’m moving on to browser navigation and logout checks. These tests make no paid provider calls or hosted database changes.\n\n> The production-build browser checks are passing so far, including back/forward restoration and delayed old-target responses. The full Jest run also passed: 1,471 tests. I’ve since added two more account/lifecycle cases and will include them in the final run.\n>\n> One limit to be clear about: aborting a browser request cannot recall data already sent to a provider. The checks verify that its late response cannot overwrite the active workspace and that the next request builds fresh, authorized context.\n\n> The full browser run found one failure outside the new Q&A tests: the target-creation test counted two “Not ingested” labels instead of one. All 30 other scenarios passed.\n>\n> I’m checking that failure against unchanged main before attributing it to 019. The temporary summary/selector check also needs their updated test fixtures copied into it; its first typecheck used older fixtures, so that result does not yet establish a compatibility problem.\n\n> The unchanged-main build reproduces the same browser failure: the selector counts a label inside a hidden, preserved page. I’ve updated that assertion to count visible labels and am rerunning it.\n\nCorrection to the preceding hypothesis, preserved as original assistant text:\n\n> The failure snapshot corrected my initial diagnosis: both labels are visible—one in the target details and one in the ingestion panel. The test’s whole-page count is stale. I’ll scope it to the ingestion status it is meant to verify. The failure still reproduces on unchanged main.\n\n## Verification and failed approaches — session notes\n\n- Read issue #70 and the supplied Sprint 2 rules: S2-BR-022, 023 and 024.\n- Read installed Next preserving-ui-state guide before changing the component.\n- Created an isolated feature worktree; preserved primary checkout and all branches.\n- Initial attempt to write tests through Python failed because Python was not\n  installed. No source edit occurred from that attempt. Switched to Node file I/O.\n- New Activity/account tests initially reported 5 failures versus 12 original\n  passing cases. Failed cases can leave mockReturnValueOnce entries queued, so\n  mockReset was added to isolate cases correctly.\n- An initial string replacement missed CRLF boundaries, leaving an undefined\n  useEffect reference. Normalized the edit input, completed layout cleanup and\n  restored passing tests. This intermediate error was not treated as a defect in\n  the original product.\n- Production build with linked review-worktree dependencies required a temporary\n  parent Turbopack root. The config was restored byte-for-byte in finally; no\n  machine-specific config change is delivered.\n- First temporary 006/017 combination failed because old test fixtures lacked\n  userId and the expanded aggregateReviews interface. Including the relevant\n  story fixture updates produced passing typecheck and 47 focused tests.\n- A direct shared-workspace patch did not apply; manually composed the summary\n  and selector in a separate unpublished worktree, preserving the Q&A panel and\n  new user scope. That composition is not delivered as another feature's code.\n- First full browser result: 30 passed, 1 failed (creation status count). The same\n  failure reproduced on an unchanged main-equivalent production build. A visible\n  filter did not solve it; the snapshot proved both labels are legitimately\n  visible. The eventual assertion targets #ingestion's status, and the isolated\n  creation test then passed.\n- Full coverage run: 80 suites, 1,473 tests, zero skips; overall lines 94.11%.\n- Changed-code gate: 32/32 production lines, 7/7 branch outcomes; PASS.\n- Three temporary faults were each caught (1, 3 and 2 failing cases); original\n  bytes restored in finally, then 70 focused tests passed on restored sources.\n- Final browser results and delivery status are recorded in the story guide.\n\n## Accepted / changed / rejected\n\nAccepted: fresh server-side context per request; reuse real S2-003 harness;\naccount-and-target-scoped UI state; Activity cleanup; request-identity checks;\nnegative concurrency tests; clear evidence and limitations.\n\nChanged: moved UI submission indicators to the current request's pending state,\nadded server-verified userId to the form key, and corrected the unrelated stale\nbrowser status assertion after reproducing it on baseline.\n\nRejected: adding another context cache/coordinator, duplicating 018's old helpers,\ncopying 006/017 production code into 019, weakening assertions to pass, declaring\nhosted/live-model behavior verified by fake infrastructure, or changing GitHub\nwithout user confirmation.\n\nAll data in tests is synthetic. No real credentials or .env.local were read.\n`);\n'@; $code | node; node node_modules/prettier/bin/prettier.cjs --write docs/s2-019-prevent-stale-context.md ai-transcripts/2026-11-15_shawn_S2-019_codex.md tests/e2e/auth/analysis-targets.spec.ts; node node_modules/prettier/bin/prettier.cjs --check .; node node_modules/eslint/bin/eslint.js .; node node_modules/typescript/bin/tsc --noEmit --incremental false","workdir":"<USER_HOME>/Documents/NJIT/Fall 26/CS490/ReviewLens-Codex-Artifacts/reviewlens-s2-019-prevent-stale-context","sandbox_permissions":"require_escalated","max_output_tokens":1400,"yield_time_ms":1000}));

S2-010/011 service contracts, token budget, quota and disabled-by-default\nconfiguration are preserved.\n\n## Validation\n\n- 80 Jest suites / 1,473 tests passed, zero skips.\n- 31/31 production-build Edge browser tests passed with local fake Supabase.\n- Formatting, lint, explicit typecheck and production build passed.\n- Changed-code coverage: 32/32 lines and 7/7 branch outcomes.\n- Three restored source-fault challenges were caught by regression tests.\n- Separate unpublished 006/017/019 composition typechecked; 47 focused tests passed.\n  Shared workspace reconciliation must retain userId={user.id} on ReviewQuestions.\n\nNo paid provider calls or hosted database changes. Browser answers are intercepted;\nprovider-boundary tests use the real adapter over fake HTTP with synthetic config.\nThis does not certify live model semantics or hosted RLS deployment. Browser abort\ncannot recall an already dispatched provider request; its late result cannot alter\nthe active form, and subsequent requests independently rebuild authorized context.\n\n## Evidence and acceptance\n\n- docs/s2-019-prevent-stale-context.md\n- ai-transcripts/2026-11-15_shawn_S2-019_codex.md\n\nAI assistance: Codex. Missing and foreign targets remain indistinguishable.\\r\\n- Render names and sources as escaped text; switch links use fixed internal routes and encoded\\r\\n  target IDs.\\r\\n- Preserve existing target-scoped Q&A context rebuilding, form reset, pending-request\\r\\n  cancellation, response-target validation, and empty-dataset restrictions.\\r\\n\\r\\nNo schema, RLS, grants, dependency versions, or provider integrations changed. No migration or dependency change.\n\n## Final integrated local validation\n\n- Full coverage run: **83 suites / 1,517 tests passed**, zero skips.\n- Combined focused security/context/client matrix: **130 tests passed**.\n- Production-build Edge auth/workspace suite: **31/31 passed**.\n- Changed-code gate: **32/32 lines**, 7/7 branch outcomes; overall lines 94.11%.\n- Formatting, lint, explicit typecheck and production build passed.\n- Original three source-fault challenges remain recorded in the evidence.\n\nTests make no paid calls or hosted changes. Browser responses are intercepted;\nprovider serialization is inspected separately through the real adapter over fake HTTP.\nAn aborted browser fetch cannot recall an already dispatched provider request, but\nits late completion cannot update the new active form. ### 2026-11-15T22:43:28.336Z - assistant (source turn 176, record 7638)

For 019, **final team acceptance means the reviewers agree the story’s required behavior is demonstrated**:

- Switching A → B → A uses only the active target’s reviews.
- Late responses cannot overwrite the current workspace.
- Back/forward, refresh and account changes don’t restore stale Q&A state.
- The implementation fits the merged Q&A flow.

We’ve already collected automated evidence for these behaviors. Reviewers can inspect it and request a short walkthrough if needed.

It’s **not an additional approval ceremony or a dependency on 006/017**. For your PR, the concrete remaining gates are current-head CI plus functional and independent security approval. A reviewer’s acceptance of the requirement evidence can cover “final team acceptance.”

Hiding frontend controls is not access protection (S1-BR-004).\\r\\n- **Authorization and ownership.** Any code that reads, writes, updates, or deletes user-owned data must enforce ownership server-side, and cross-user operations must be denied (S1-BR-008). Row Level Security must be enabled on all tables holding user data, and its policies must restrict access to the owning user.\\r\\n- **Ingestion.** Any code that parses uploaded CSV/JSON or writes review data, including validation, size limits, and ownership of the target.\\r\\n- **Database access.** SQL, migrations, Row Level Security policies, and any use of a privileged or service-role key.\\r\\n- **Secrets and configuration.** Environment variables, provider credentials, database credentials, review-service credentials, and anything that could end up in client-side code. `.env` files must be in `.gitignore`. Only values a provider explicitly designs to be public (such as the Supabase anon key, exposed through `NEXT_PUBLIC_` variables) may reach the browser. The Supabase service-role key and the OpenAI key stay server-side only (S1-BR-011).\\r\\n- **Third-party calls.** Code that calls the OpenAI API or another external service, especially what data is sent.\\r\\n- **New or upgraded dependencies** with network, file-system, or install-script access.\\r\\n- **Error handling.** Responses must not leak stack traces, secret keys, or internal diagnostics
